# MVP completion audit, 2026-10-11

This audit covers HEAD `2560eead` at upstream pin `13c98575`. Each of the 15 requirement areas and 11 product loops had its own read-only research agent. A completeness critic then reviewed their findings, and a planner grouped the tasks. All percentages are estimates by the auditing agents, and they are checked against the code, not taken from the ledgers. The raw per-agent data is kept with the session.

**Overall MVP completion: 47%.** Software completion: 79%.

Alpha Phone at HEAD 2560eead (upstream pin 13c98575) is about 79% software-complete and about 47% MVP-complete. The average of the per-item overall figures is 48; release, AP-14 and the owner-gated AP-06/MVP-52 scope pull it down. The acceptance evidence is thin. Class E exists only for developer APKs with an unpackaged runtime, on one API 36 emulator, at 2961bd9e/9a1ca161. There is no distributable B build, no class I evidence, no real-integration R evidence and no device D evidence at the pin.

Two critic findings were checked against source, with different results:
- **Confirmed:** LOOP-B note-origins. AlphaConnectionPlugin.secureCompareExchange (around line 130) has no 'note-origins:v1:device' entry, so on Android a reminder saved from a note never keeps its link to the note.
- **Refuted:** CRITIC 'notes-draft' / AP-10. The same allowlist already contains name.equals(AlphaCredentialStore.NOTES_DRAFT), and AlphaCredentialStore.java:18 sets NOTES_DRAFT="notes-draft:v1:device". RendererCredentialSlots does not block 'notes-draft', so the Android encrypted Notes draft path is admitted. AP-10 goes back to about 58 overall and 85 software; LOOP-B is about 50 overall and 78 software. The CRITIC task is reduced to regression coverage inside the slot-consistency test.

The 202 software tasks are deduplicated into 10 packages with disjoint file ownership:
- **WP1:** Java code under android/app/src/main, plus the renderer files for notes trash, context selection and note sources.
- **WP2:** all androidTest code, the instrumentation registry and the runners.
- **WP3:** Home, layout, accessibility, Settings, Maps and document-selection UI, plus template.html.
- **WP4:** the agent and connection renderer.
- **WP5:** voice, Clock, scan and the Calendar draft.
- **WP6:** Inbox, the browser renderer, digests and workflows.
- **WP7:** test-only flag-off journey specs that own the shared flag-off harness files.
- **WP8:** host-runtime scripts and heavy builds.
- **WP9:** release, supply chain and bundle audit, plus the manifest, Gradle files and AOSP.
- **WP10:** ledger and docs. It runs last.

Packages 1 to 9 are independent. A few tasks are split into native and renderer halves, designed so either half is safe to merge first: clock repeat days, J05 paste fallback, Gmail cumulative scopes and the AP-07 inbox stale state. AP-06 send policy is owner-gated (MVP-01). It is implemented behind a single policy constant that keeps today's auto-send default.

## Rationale and stale ledger claims

### AP-01: Distinct branded home, app grid and assistant entry; independent package and storage identity

The software for this item is nearly complete. These are in source: the branded Home, the All-apps drawer with search, favorites and order, launches by exact component and profile, refresh on package change, the landscape layout, the ASSIST entry activity, the launcher flavor's HOME/DEFAULT filter, and a package and storage identity of its own. On the package side, applicationId comes from app.config.json (ai.elizaresearch.alphaphone), app_name is a resValue, and scripts/apk.mjs checks the package and the HOME filter for each variant. Renderer storage keys use alpha.* or alphaphone:* prefixes.

The ledgers are behind the code. requirements.json and mvp-current-status.md say no emulator HOME-role run is recorded. docs/verification.md does record one at 2961bd9e/9a1ca161, class E, on developer APKs with an unpackaged runtime: LauncherHome passed 21 of 22 and then 3 of 3, including the three-installed-apps method, and SettingsRoles passed 8 of 8 and 3 of 3.

Acceptance evidence is thin:
- LauncherLibraryInstrumentedTest and AssistantInstrumentedTest have never run.
- Accessibility fails on Home at 200% font (standalone: the last row of apps sits under the composer) and fails on the launcher's click step.
- Rotation has passed only at an earlier pin.
- No distributable APK, AOSP image (I) or physical-device (D) HOME evidence exists.
- Real work profiles and real install or remove have not been exercised.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/requirements.json AP-01 'remaining' says 'no emulator HOME-role run is recorded'. False: docs/verification.md 'Emulator results at 2961bd9e' records LauncherHome and SettingsRoles passing under --home-role (class E), and round 7 (6dcdde34) recorded LauncherHome 4/4.
- docs/mvp-current-status.md 'Home and launcher' row says LauncherHomeInstrumentedTest and SettingsRolesInstrumentedTest 'have no recorded run' and the evidence column says 'S; E historical only'. Stale: both have a run at the current pin.
- docs/mvp-current-status.md 'Home and launcher' row says the drawer 'launches one by package'. Outdated: home-launcher.ts and LauncherLibrary.java launch by package, activity and user.
- docs/mvp-current-status.md and docs/decisions.md A-22 say RotationInstrumentedTest 'has no recorded run'. False: docs/verification.md records Rotation passing in round 7 (6dcdde34) and at 73b973a5, at the earlier pin 40dbe96bd1. It has not run at pin 13c98575.
- docs/mvp-remaining-work-2026-10-09.md MVP-53 'Blocked on' says 'the HOME-role campaign ... never run', and 'Remaining' lists 'return from three native apps' as open. The HOME-role campaign ran and LauncherHome's three-installed-apps method passed on the emulator (class E). Only LauncherLibraryInstrumentedTest has never run.
- docs/mvp-remaining-work-2026-10-09.md 'MVP-53 software status' bullet says the Android sources were 'compiled with javac against the SDK only: no Gradle build, ... APK'. A later bullet in the same section (Integration, 2026-10-10) says Gradle built APKs. The two contradict each other.
- MVP-53 Remaining says LauncherHomeInstrumentedTest 'matches the exact label Open Settings and needs checking on an image with two Settings entries'. Still true in source (LauncherHomeInstrumentedTest.java lines 107-112) and unaddressed.
- AGENTS.md still names patches/eliza as the patch route, but the patches directory no longer exists (noted under MVP-07). [Historical audit finding; resolved on 2026-10-12, see Implementation results.]

### AP-02: Standalone and HOME APKs from one source; offline shell; stock settings and recovery access

Software (S) is largely complete and the code matches the claims. One Gradle source produces two flavors, standalone and launcher, and only the launcher adds the HOME/DEFAULT filter (android/app/src/launcher/AndroidManifest.xml). verify-apks checks all four APKs: the HOME filter per flavor, the flag-off bundle audit, one shared signer, one version, the packaged runtime and speech qualification. Font and licence findings are flagged only, because UNRESOLVED_FONT_LICENCE_BLOCKS_DISTRIBUTION=false. The renderer is bundled, and Welcome offers "Use local apps without AI", which is saved across restart. Settings offers "Make Alpha your Home app" (the result is read back from getStatus, never assumed) and "Default apps in Android". MainActivity recreates itself with a bounded backoff when the renderer dies, and keeps one live instance across HOME and standard task starts. Remaining software gaps: (1) nothing tests renderer-loss recovery; (2) there is no native escape surface when the renderer keeps dying while Alpha is HOME; (3) handleOnDestroy has not been reviewed across all plugins after the a326b8dd crash; (4) the ledgers are stale. A distribution build is blocked by inputs outside the repo: the reviewed embedding host, qualified speech (A-24/MVP-23) and the signer (A-06; distributable=true needs a signed release). Acceptance: no distribution B build exists at pin 13c9857598, only developer APKs. Class E exists at the current pin, but only for developer APKs with an unpackaged runtime and a dirty runtime-manifest: on the launcher APK, LauncherHome passed 21 of 22 runs (APKs A) and 3 of 3 (APKs B) under --home-role; SettingsRoles passed 8 of 8 and 3 of 3 after the test fix; on both flavors Shell, StartupReadiness and LocalAgentOfflineApps passed; in the default campaign BrowserFlow failed its live-search method, so that run exits 1. There is no LauncherLibrary or Rotation run, no class I boot-time HOME, and no class D phone run of HOME, recovery or emergency routes. The result files are not committed.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/requirements.json AP-02 'remaining' says no developer APK 'was installed' and that the Welcome local-apps entry is covered only by a stubbed-bridge browser test. Round 8 (docs/verification.md, 'Emulator results at 2961bd9e') installed developer APKs built at the current pin and ran LocalAgentOfflineAppsInstrumentedTest, which passed on both flavors (class E). Only device (class D) evidence is missing. requirements.json was last edited in 3aeb9f99, before the round 8 record 0acfffd9.
- docs/mvp-current-status.md line 69 (Home and launcher) says LauncherHomeInstrumentedTest and SettingsRolesInstrumentedTest 'have no recorded run (class E)' and gives the evidence class as 'S; E historical only'. verification.md records both passing under --home-role at the current pin, on developer APKs.
- docs/mvp-current-status.md line 90 (Release) says none of the developer builds 'was installed' and gives the evidence class as 'B historical only (developer APKs at earlier pins)'. Developer APKs were built at the current pin (hosted run 38118499272 at c0b19147, dc58b4b2, and 2961bd9e/9a1ca161) and installed on the round 8 emulator.
- docs/mvp-remaining-work-2026-10-09.md and its .json, MVP-20 'Current': 'No APK build is recorded at the current pin, so nothing is installed ... No run is recorded at the current source.' Both are false since round 8. Its 'Blocked on: EMULATOR: default campaign and --home-role' is partly met for developer APKs.
- MVP-53 'Blocked on' says 'the HOME-role campaign ... [has] never run'. The HOME-role campaign ran in round 8. LauncherLibraryInstrumentedTest has still never run, so that part is true.
- docs/core-loop-audit.md line 769 says LauncherHome has 'registry entries (this audit), with no run'. It has since run (round 8).
- The AP-02 gate 'four distribution APKs ... pass scripts/verify-apks.mjs' is ambiguous. verify-apks exits 0 on unsigned or developer releases and only records distributable:false. distributable:true also needs a release signed by android/release-signer.json (A-06), so the AP-02 B gate depends on signing, which the entry does not say.
- AGENTS.md still tells agents to use 'an explicit tested patch in patches/eliza'. The patches directory does not exist (already listed under MVP-07 but not fixed). [Historical audit finding; resolved on 2026-10-12, see Implementation results.]

### AP-03: Eliza sign-in and pairing with approved routing adapter and owner-scoped sessions

Checked against the code, the source side of AP-03 is mostly in place, but almost none of the required acceptance evidence exists at the current pin. Production Android signs in to Eliza Cloud through a CLI session that it polls (cloud-protocol.ts login). Before starting the resident agent it checks the identity and the credit balance and requires both to come from the same credential (admitCloudResident in connection-ui.tsx). Pairing is retired in two places: in the renderer (remotePairingRetired and connectRemote at line 278) and in the native transport (ConnectionRoutes.retiredPairing and admittedOrigin, applied in AlphaConnectionPlugin.request before any connection opens). Cloud sign-out sends DELETE /api/v1/api-keys/current once and reports "revoked" only when Cloud confirms it. Remote and Cloud sessions are tied to an origin, owner and agent, and native local-agent calls reject a changed owner (AlphaLocalAgentPlugin lines 600-703). Coverage is source-class only: JVM tests of ConnectionRoutes (test/connection-routes.test.mjs plus test/fixtures/ConnectionRoutesTest.java) and flag-off Playwright specs against a stubbed bridge (connection-boundaries.production.spec.ts, saved-signin-retry.production.spec.ts, native-cloud-credits.spec.ts). Software gaps found: (1) no instrumented test runs the transport's route and origin guards in a flag-off APK, because the ConnectionInstrumentedTest network cases assume a test-mocks build; (2) signing in again overwrites the saved Cloud key without revoking the old one; (3) in a flag-off build, openExternal still accepts any *.eliza.app host, staging included; (4) no flag-off test covers replacing one Cloud account with another on Android; (5) the PRD and requirements text still describe pairing and pairing callbacks. Acceptance: there is no APK, emulator, real-integration or device evidence for AP-03 at the current pin. No live Eliza Cloud sign-in, credit check or revocation has run. The Connection class runs only in the dispatch-only resident-android.yml recovery-ui job, and no passing run is recorded.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/requirements.json AP-03 'gate' still asks for wrong-origin and callback tests framed around pairing and a managed endpoint. Pairing is retired on production Android, and sign-in polls a CLI session with no redirect callback. The gate should name what actually has to be tested: the native origin and route guards in an APK, CLI-session expiry and reuse, self-revocation, and replacing one Cloud account with another.
- docs/requirements.json AP-03 'evidence' leaves out the files that actually enforce the boundary: android/.../ConnectionRoutes.java, test/connection-routes.test.mjs, test/fixtures/ConnectionRoutesTest.java, test/browser/connection-boundaries.production.spec.ts and apps/app/src/runtime/local-agent.ts (bindResidentCloudProvider).
- docs/prd.md line 15 ('Pair the device to the correct Alpha agent'), line 39 ('shell → pair → agent message') and line 47 ('use upstream pairing') conflict with pairing being retired on production Android (PR 379; mvp-current-status 'Connection choices').
- The docs/decisions.md A-02 rows (lines 45 and 71) still describe 'owner/agent pairing' and 'wrong-origin' tests as the acceptance for AP-03, although Android has no pairing route any more.
- The requirement title says 'owner-scoped credentials/sessions'. On Android the Cloud credential is used for billing only and is not scoped to the resident owner identity. Conversations stay keyed to the local resident owner when the Cloud account changes, and nothing documents or tests whether that is intended.
- No ledger records that signing in to Cloud again replaces the saved key without revoking the previous one (cloud-protocol.ts login → credentials.write). Only an explicit sign-out revokes.

### AP-04: Android-resident agent execution with hosted Qwen inference on Cerebras, honest offline state

Source (class S) is largely in place. The native resident lifecycle in AlphaLocalAgentPlugin.java has start, cancelStart, stop, status, attach, request and stream. The model is pinned to qwen-3.8-27b in both the Cerebras and Eliza Cloud provider routes (local-agent.ts:255, AlphaLocalAgentPlugin.java:157/256/353). A Cerebras key is checked against /v1/models before it is saved. Stream drops are recovered idempotently under clientMessageId (local-agent.ts:211-236). Stop goes through the upstream turn-abort route. Production Android gets a separate "local apps without AI" offline choice, and the Settings privacy row says "Offline · no hosted inference requests" when nothing is connected. The source gaps that remain are smaller. (1) Typed upstream failure kinds (insufficient_credits, provider_issue, generation_timeout, no_provider) all collapse into one generic message, so loss of credits or of the network mid-session is not reported honestly. (2) Stale "pair again" wording reaches production Android. (3) The Cloud-provider admission code (applyProviderEnvironment for elizacloud, cloudProviderToken expiry, bind and rollback) has no test at all, only the Cerebras branch is tested. (4) The model label is inconsistent between Settings and Models. (5) The status line in on-device-agent-plan.md is stale. Acceptance evidence is thin. The only emulator (E) evidence at the pin is LocalAgentOfflineApps passing on developer APKs with an unpackaged runtime (round 8, 2961bd9e). No APK with the staged resident runtime has been built at the pin (MVP-39). ResidentService, ResidentEgressRedaction, the process-kill classes and AssistantResidentReuse have no recorded run. There is no real Eliza Cloud or Cerebras round trip (R) and no device run (D). The core claim of this requirement, resident execution with real hosted inference surviving restart and reconnect, is therefore unproven above source level.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/on-device-agent-plan.md:47 says 'Both distribution builds and scoped native IPC... campaigns have passed at the revisions recorded in the current acceptance ledger'. The current ledger (docs/verification.md, round 8 at pin 13c98575) records only developer APKs with an unpackaged runtime, and MVP-39 states that no build with the staged resident runtime exists at the pin.
- docs/on-device-agent-plan.md:47 says 'Full resident workflow execution remains blocked by a diagnosed SIGSYS worker exit before model RPC'. No other ledger mentions SIGSYS, and commit cef69b7c ('Preserve owned seccomp compatibility handler during Bun spawn') suggests it was addressed. The blocker's current status is unrecorded either way.
- apps/app/src/runtime/connection-ui.tsx:231 and :1315 show 'Sign in or pair again.' and 'Your session has expired. Sign in or pair again.' Pairing is retired on production Android, yet the resident send path (line 1315) can show 'Sign in or pair again.', and the native panel remaps only the exact errorMessage({status:401}) string (line 1467).
- MVP-04 and MVP-34 call direct Cerebras a separate development or retained profile, but the native configureProvider method (AlphaLocalAgentPlugin.java:234) is gated only by runtimePackaged(), not by the test-mocks flag. Production native code still admits a direct Cerebras key if called; only the UI hides the option.
- MVP-21 says 'Host and JVM fixtures exercise admission, owner context and credential retirement'. No test in test/ or androidTest references applyProviderEnvironment for elizacloud, cloudProviderToken, bindCloudProvider, compareExchangeProviderAdmission or rollbackProviderAdmission. LocalAgentProviderInstrumentedTest covers only the Cerebras branch and has no recorded run.
- The PRD criterion 'no inference-available claim without health evidence' holds only in a weak form. Nothing claims readiness, but there is no provider health probe for the Cloud route. 'Connected · On this device' follows a credit-balance read, not a model round trip, and mid-session provider failures are not classified (see the failure-kind task).

### AP-05: Pill, input, overlay and full assistant modes with keyboard and navigation continuity

Software (S): all four modes are wired in production code. The states are hidden (pill), input, sheet (overlay) and full (model.js ~5535-5602; chat-overlay-motion-adapter.ts types Mode = hidden|input|sheet|full). Swipe, grabber, tap and Back transitions work. Back steps full->sheet->rest (model.js back() 5253). Both activities use adjustResize, and MainActivity applies the IME inset to the decor padding (MainActivity.java ~262). The renderer resizes on visualViewport (main.tsx 168-191). The durable draft lives in AssistantDraftController, the transcript scroll anchor in model.js 5056-5090, and the assistant surface opens in full. Browser specs cover pre-dispatch retention and draft across synthetic Home, Back, a keyboard-sized resize and reload: chat-continuity.spec.ts:286, compact-viewport.spec.ts:30, native-insets.spec.ts, message-actions.spec.ts:91.
Gaps:
- Every system Back (even full->sheet) and every HOME calls alphaClient.cancel() and aborts the in-flight foreground reply (agent-adapter.ts ~476-481). No test covers this.
- No test covers the transcript scroll anchor across resize or Back.
- No native test drives real Back/HOME with the IME up.
- The assistant activity has no IME check.
- Home at 200% font lets the floating composer cover the last grid row, which fails the Accessibility test on standalone.
Acceptance: class E exists only for ShellInstrumentedTest. It passed on both flavors at 2961bd9e and 9a1ca161 (developer APKs with an unpackaged runtime), so the composer staying above a real IME on MainActivity is proven at class E. Assistant, Flow, AssistantResidentReuse and Rotation have never run. Accessibility fails on both flavors. There is no D, I or R evidence.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/mvp-remaining-work-2026-10-09.md/.json MVP-22 'Blocked on': 'Shell includes a soft-keyboard layout check ... none has a recorded run' is wrong: ShellInstrumentedTest (with the IME check) passed both flavors at 2961bd9e and 9a1ca161 (docs/verification.md 'Emulator results at 2961bd9e', also round 7 at 6dcdde34). Assistant and Flow indeed have no recorded run.
- docs/requirements.json AP-05 gate and docs/mvp-current-status.md AP-05 row ('E: soft-keyboard resize ... on an installed APK') do not record that the MainActivity soft-keyboard composer check already has class E passes; the remaining E is real Back/HOME continuity with IME, the assistant surface (AlphaAssistActivity) and overlays.
- docs/core-loop-audit.md 'Stale ledger statements' table (~757, ~761) still lists the AP-05 gate 'S: composer draft survives every pre-dispatch failure' as current; the ledgers have since been corrected, so the audit table itself is stale.
- MVP-22 'Current' says browser tests cover draft retention 'across Home, Back and a viewport resize' — true only for an idle chat with synthetic events; Back/Home during a streaming reply aborts the reply (agent-adapter.ts backHandler/homeHandler call alphaClient.cancel()) and is untested.
- PRD AP-05 requires 'draft/scroll state survives resize and Back/Home'; no test asserts transcript scroll position across resize or Back/Home (only draft value and composer in viewport).

### AP-06: Voice entry and cancellation with explicit microphone state and typing fallback (speech runtime, MVP-01/05/23/24)

Software: the voice surfaces are largely built and have strong class-S coverage. Flag-off builds route through Eliza Cloud (apps/app/src/runtime/voice-selection.ts). There are explicit recorder states (ready/recording/transcribing/review/error, plus denied with an 'Open app settings' recovery), a 'Switch to keyboard' typing fallback in chat, signed-out refusal with no capture, and cancellation and retirement of held ASR/TTS/navigation. These are covered by browser-speech, chat-voice-mode, voice-owned-navigation, production-surface and voice-denied-recovery tests, and the native permission campaign tests exist. Four things hold the score down. (1) The policy conflict between P-01 and P-07 is open: an ongoing Cloud conversation sends each spoken turn, against P-01's record, review, then send. (2) The latency marks are not wired in: beginVoiceTurn is never called anywhere in apps/app/src, so every markVoiceTiming call in local-speech-playback.ts returns false and production never produces a timing record; the four manual-entry tests are test.todo. (3) An empty or silent Cloud transcript surfaces a misleading 'check microphone access' error and offers no typing choice. (4) Some code is dead or unreachable in production: cloudSpeechDisclosure(), and setSpeakReplies, which has no UI. Acceptance: the denied, revoked and limit microphone campaigns passed only at the round 7 source (6dcdde34, older pin) and were not rerun at pin 13c98575. On-device speech functional acceptance is failed (arm64-v8a 1 of 2; x86_64 never executed), and the requalified candidate is not admitted. There is no real Cloud transcription (R), no physical microphone/speaker/Bluetooth campaign (D) and no latency sample. Overall: the owner decisions MVP-01, A-24 and A-10 gate most of what remains.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/requirements.json AP-06 'remaining' says 'The denied- and revoked-microphone campaigns have no recorded run.' This is wrong: docs/verification.md (round 7 at 6dcdde34) records `test-native-permissions.mjs voice` and `voice-revoke` passed on both flavors, and the 73b973a5 section records voice-revoke passed and voice failed. What is true is that they were not rerun at the current pin 13c98575 (the 2961bd9e section lists 'the other permission scenarios' as not run).
- MVP-23 Current says the runtime rebuild 'is not on main'. But android/local-speech/requalification/candidate-20261009.json and the manifest's requalificationCandidate block (arm64 emulator passed, x86_64 not executed, not admitted) are on main. The entry should describe the candidate that is on main and the pin it was built at.
- MVP-23 'Blocked on: EMULATOR' and docs/mvp-current-status.md's evidence column 'D failed on arm64-v8a' describe different evidence classes. The committed failure is from a physical Android 16 ARM64 device (manifest nativeExecution.deviceClass); the candidate pass is emulator-only. The ledgers should keep the two apart.
- docs/mvp-current-status.md 'Speech and recording' row still says 'Browser-build speech recognition is in scope; any cloud route requires disclosed opt-in'. That contradicts P-07 and voice-selection.ts, which make Cloud the unconditional flag-off default for the browser build too. The PRD AP-06 row (docs/prd.md:28, 'local first ... cloud route an explicit disclosed opt-in') has the same conflict; it is marked pending MVP-01 elsewhere but the row text itself is unqualified.
- MVP-05 implies the timing helpers only lack entry-point marks. In fact beginVoiceTurn is never called anywhere in apps/app/src, so the existing markVoiceTiming('send'/'first-token'/'final-reply'/'first-audio') calls in apps/app/src/prototype/local-speech-playback.ts always return false: production records no timing at all, not even helper-level records.
- voice-selection.ts exports cloudSpeechDisclosure() and chooseVoiceRoute() as if a disclosed Cloud opt-in exists. cloudSpeechDisclosure has no caller in apps/app/src, and Settings shows only 'Eliza Cloud (uses credits)' (settings-adapter.ts:333).
- docs/local-agent-voice-validation.md:16-17 and docs/standalone-paired-asr.md:40 cite patches/eliza files; the patches/ directory does not exist. AGENTS.md:6 also still names patches/eliza. [Historical audit finding; resolved on 2026-10-12, see Implementation results.]

### AP-07: Daily overview with real agenda, attention items and briefs (digests MVP-19/38)

I checked the requirement against the code. The renderer side of AP-07 is real and wired into the production build. In apps/app/src/prototype/data-adapter.ts, Home renders four cards: Calendar (with overdue reminders), Workflows, Inbox and a Latest brief card. The text for every card comes from apps/app/src/prototype/home-cards.ts. main.tsx registers inboxAttention and latestRetainedDigest through setHomeSources. In the flag-off build the fixture ATTENTION_ROWS and HOME_DEFAULTS are empty (fixtures.empty.js), so Home shows no fake avatars and no fake brief. There are unit tests (test/home-cards.test.mjs), development-lane browser specs and production-lane browser specs, including home-brief-result.production.spec.ts, which runs from a digest result through both result routes. Software is therefore about 85%. It is held back by a few real gaps. An overdue reminder still hides the next event. presentHomeAttention's count and text are computed but never rendered, and its stale label can claim "0 unread when last read" for an inbox that was never read. No calendar adapter publishes a real read time, so the "Read <time>" label is the time Home first rendered the rows. The production Inbox card also has no explicit stale state, although PRD AP-07 requires loading, empty, stale and error states. Acceptance is about 5%. Everything at pin 13c9857598 is class S, with the Android bridge stubbed in the browser specs. None of these exist at that pin: an APK with the resident runtime, an emulator run of HostedProcessRestart or the calendar classes, a real Gmail, CalendarProvider or scheduled-digest run, or a TalkBack or device run. The one digest-restart host run was made at an older pin. The requirement's own gate is R then D. Overall is about 45%: the software is largely done, but the gate evidence is almost entirely missing and is blocked on A-09, MVP-34 and MVP-35.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/mvp-remaining-work-2026-10-09.md MVP-38 says the digest-restart host check ran 'at upstream 352d7a08'. docs/verification.md:279 records it at pin 40dbe96b. docs/mvp-current-status.md 'Digests and schedules' also says 352d7a08. The ledgers disagree, and neither run is at the current pin 13c9857598.
- docs/core-loop-audit.md:765 says AP-07 evidence lists only dev-digest-schedule.spec.ts. requirements.json now lists ten evidence paths, so that audit row is stale.
- docs/core-loop-status.md:148 says 'with an agent connected the Home card lists workflows'. Home now has a separate Latest brief card (data-alpha-home-brief) that shows the retained brief alongside the Workflows card, so the parenthetical is out of date.
- docs/daily-overview.md says the calendar card shows 'Read <time>' as when this app read the source. No adapter sets calendarState.nativeCalendarReadAt (grep finds only the reader in data-adapter.ts:98). The time is always the first Home render that saw new rows, an approximation the doc does not state. The same applies to the reminder read time.
- docs/daily-overview.md and home-cards.ts describe presentHomeAttention's stale, error and loading text and count as Home card states. In production the template never binds homeAttentionCount or homeAttentionText (0 occurrences in template.html), and inbox-cloud-adapter overrides homeAttentionLabel. Only attention.action (goTriage) is live. The 'stale' attention state therefore never reaches the user, and the Inbox card has no explicit stale state as PRD AP-07 requires.
- docs/requirements.json and mvp-current-status note the pin as 13c9857598, which matches upstream.lock.json and the committed gitlink. The working checkout of vendor/eliza is at 3cca1ee4 (git status shows 'M vendor/eliza'). Any local run would therefore not be at the recorded pin; this is probably concurrent Codex work.

### AP-09: Inbox/Gmail and Calendar (MVP-12/14/26/35/36)

Software (S): every file the requirement names as evidence exists and is substantial. That covers the Inbox Cloud adapter (609 lines), gmail-mailbox, calendar-adapter, calendar-availability, inbox-operation, use-in-email-review and the upstream google-gmail-seam. There are browser, node and JVM tests for reviewed send, notifications, hostile attachments, Use in email (development lane and flag-off) and calendar availability. No TODO, FIXME or stubs turned up in the AP-09 sources. What the software still lacks: label management (A-15 option c, if chosen), Google/Cloud calendars (by design only CalendarProvider and the in-app calendar are shown), an instrumented or emulator path for Use in email and for the availability review inside the WebView, and the upstream availability read in plugin-native-calendar.

Acceptance: the ledgers are behind here. The round 8 emulator record (docs/verification.md, "Emulator results at 2961bd9e", pin 13c98575987a, developer APKs) shows these passing on both flavors: CalendarAvailabilityInstrumentedTest (4 tests, its first run), CalendarCrud, CalendarAgentCrud (both methods), CalendarFlow, CalendarRange, CalendarTruncation, InboxOperationJournal, MailAttachment, and test-native-restart.mjs inbox. That meets the E half of the AP-09 gate at emulator level. The R half is wholly open. The Google code exchange still returns 401 (docs/cloud-production-validation.md), so no real mailbox, managed route deployment or real send exists. There is also no D evidence (synced account, TalkBack, device), and Use in email has not run on an APK.

Overall: the software is mostly complete. MVP-35, the P0 item, is an external operator fix and gates MVP-36, MVP-14 acceptance and the Inbox parts of other items.

### AP-10: Browser, camera, photos, maps, notes, files, reminders/clock, passwords (MVP-15/25/27-31)

Software (class S) is broad and largely complete. Browser: AlphaBrowserPlugin and BrowserDownloads give persistent and private tabs, downloads, pop-ups and app links. Passwords: vault and Autofill come from upstream plugin-native-passwords plus apps/app/src/passwords, and the dev vault is gated and listed in the bundle audit. Camera, scan and OCR: camera-adapter.ts has native capturePhoto, the scan builder and local-ocr. Also present are Photos, Files tree, Notes Trash with the full-Trash dialog and permanent-deletion backstop, Reminders and the Clock handoff, and Maps with regional provider and J04. Each area has many Playwright and node specs, and every instrumented class exists and is wired to the runner or a campaign. What keeps software short of 100: (1) no production Maps provider. regional-provider.ts sets baseUrl from VITE_MAPS_BASE_URL, which is unset, so a flag-off build cannot route; this waits on decision A-23. (2) Known races remain (MVP-15): the native permanent-deletion pass against a live renderer, and the stale-view and cross-tab cases in the browser build. (3) Alpha has no instrumented test of its own for vault save, unlock and native app fill. PasswordBrowserFill only checks that web forms are refused. (4) NotesSecureStorage's migration method has no campaign. (5) BrowserFlow's live-search method requires www.google.com, so the default emulator run fails. Acceptance: class E evidence exists at near-HEAD source 2961bd9e (round 8), the only later product change being a326b8dd (problem-log rejection in AlphaDevicePlugin). Passed there: NotesTrashBackstop 9/9, NotesStorageDurability, ReminderLifecycle, ClockHandoff, ClockRepeatDays, CameraScan, PasswordBrowserFill, FilesTree, BrowserSignins, BrowserContinuity, and the notes and document restart campaigns. Round 7 at 6dcdde34 added test-mocks BrowserDownload and BrowserAutofill, RealClock on API 35, the reminder one-off and recovery campaigns, signin restart and camera permissions. Still never run or failing: BrowserReading, BrowserPageQuestion and BrowserReadingNavigation (failing for environment reasons, since this WebView has no isolated-world injection); PhotoEdit, PhotoFilter, PhotosBatch, PhotosMultiShare, BrowserShare, BrowserStorageFormat and BrowserDialogLifecycle (no recorded run); Maps native recovery and background navigation (never run; foreground navigation passed only in historical Build59 and Build60). There is no I, R or D evidence: no production maps gateway, no release-signed device, no real camera or OCR documents, and no audible alarm. The R and D items the requirement gate names all wait on owner decisions A-23, A-06, A-11, A-12 and A-14.

Accounts (Eliza Cloud and the Device accounts handoff), Connections (Gmail status, confirmed disconnect with read-back), Agent connection, theme (persisted under alpha.appearance.v1 and read at startup in main.tsx) and the native text-scale slider are real. Renderer and host tests are broad: settings-truth.production, settings.production, settings-device-facts, privacy-per-connection, appearance-storage, settings-page-accessibility, test/settings-truth.test.mjs, test/shade-tile-facts.test.mjs and test/diagnostics-export.test.mjs. In round 8 (2961bd9e/9a1ca161, current pin 13c9857598) SettingsSystemFacts, SettingsRoles, Notifications, TextScale and channels passed. Still missing at the current pin: SettingsFlow, the settings (SettingsNative) and notice-denied campaigns, the text-scale restart phase and SettingsCrashLog (no runner exists). ### AP-14: Signed AOSP image and update/rollback path for one phone SKU (MVP-39..43, blocked)

What the repo has is gating code. scripts/stage-aosp.mjs refuses test-mock and non-distributable APKs, and --production builds the provisioning overlay. scripts/release-blockers.mjs names each blocker. scripts/verify-apks.mjs checks releases against android/release-signer.json. scripts/pilot-update.mjs does an in-place upgrade with signer and versionCode checks and per-domain readback on debug builds. scripts/test-installed-upgrade.mjs runs domain upgrades on an emulator. Node tests cover all of these (verify-apks.test, release-signing.test, pilot-units.test, aosp-provisioning-overlay.test). Several things do not exist in Alpha: an AOSP source lock or product configuration, an Alpha brand config for the upstream build-aosp, generate-release-keys and publish-update-manifest tooling, any OTA update manifest, any rollback path (pilot-update states it is not offered), a release-build data-export readback, and a verified App Links filter. The manifest only has the custom alphaphone://cloud-delegation scheme, and assetlinks is still a placeholder template. On evidence: signerSha256 is "unset", every release APK is unsigned, there is no strict PACKAGED distribution build at the pin (MVP-39), no Cuttlefish or Pixel 10 image build or boot (MVP-41), and no signed update or recovery drill (MVP-42). The only met piece is MVP-43 (Denton rights on the owner's statement). The core blockers are external: A-01 (SKU and hardware, Linux AOSP builder) and A-06 (signer and rollback owner). Some software preparation can still be done ahead of them.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/android-and-aosp.md:129 says 'Then validate signed OTA and rollback', as if a path exists. Alpha has no OTA manifest, update channel or rollback mechanism. pilot-update.mjs's own header says rollback is not offered (pending A-06).
- docs/requirements.json AP-14 lists only three evidence entries (stage-aosp.mjs, android-and-aosp.md, native-app-distribution.md). It omits the code that carries the gate: scripts/release-blockers.mjs, android/release-signer.json, scripts/verify-apks.mjs, scripts/pilot-update.mjs and scripts/test-installed-upgrade.mjs.
- AP-14's 'remaining' field says only 'No selected phone SKU and no controlled release key'. It leaves out the missing software: no Alpha AOSP product or source lock, no OTA or rollback design, no release-build data readback (pilot-update.mjs:20 'in-app export check (not built yet)'), and no verified App Links intent filter.
- MVP-43 is listed under AP-14 as a P0 blocker, but its own text says it is met on the owner's statement. Counting it as an open AP-14 blocker overstates the remaining work.
- stage-aosp.mjs prints 'Supply --apk PATH to a signed launcher APK', but --development accepts any debuggable APK. The message is accurate only for production.

### AP-15: Accessibility and resilience (MVP-48)

The renderer side is substantial and checked against the code. test/browser/accessibility-audit.ts is a dependency-free rule set that has its own self-test (accessibility-audit.spec.ts). It is swept across every retained view in live, fixture and recovery states, in light and dark, at 200% text and in 915x412 landscape (accessibility-sweep.spec.ts), and again over the flag-off bundle (accessibility-sweep.production.spec.ts). Supporting specs are accessibility-resilience, large-text-overlap, home-landscape, notifications-handle and about 20 per-dialog accessibility specs. Several things are still open in software:
- At 200% font in the standalone flavor, the last row of Home apps sits under the floating composer, and the emulator flags this as a reading-order failure.
- A toast raised while a top-layer dialog is open is not announced.
- The Camera and Photos header titles are cut short with an ellipsis at 200%.
- Landscape combined with 200% text is not swept.
- The Android HOME (launcher) presentation is not swept.
- Nothing reports a focused control that sits under the composer.
- In the instrumented test, the ACTION_CLICK step cannot pass on WebView 133.

Native tests exist: AccessibilityInstrumentedTest, TextScaleInstrumentedTest, RotationInstrumentedTest, plus the process-death, offline and restart classes. Emulator evidence (class E) from the round 8 run at 2961bd9e:
- Passed: TextScale on both flavors; LauncherHome (repeated HOME, 3 of 3 runs with APKs B); LocalAgentOfflineApps; several restart campaigns.
- Failed: Accessibility, on both flavors and at two different steps.
- Not run: Rotation.

No device evidence (class D) exists: no TalkBack, Switch Access, physical large font, rotation, gesture navigation or full-storage run. A-22 (landscape policy) and A-25 (calendar deletion recoverability) are still undecided. Because MVP-48's done-when is independent task completion on selected hardware, the acceptance score is low.

Gradle includes the shared notifications and owned-media modules (android/settings.gradle:33-36), and AlphaPhotosPlugin, WorkflowNoticeDelivery and AlphaNotificationListener import them. ResidentAttachment.java, test/resident-attachment.test.mjs, test/installed-upgrade-runner.test.mjs and test/native-campaign-evidence.test.mjs all exist. The runner registry covers AssistantResidentReuse, WorkflowApprovalNotice, the media classes and ActionJournal. WorkflowNoticeTap and WorkflowNoticeProcessDeath are reached through scripts/ci/pending-recovery-ui.py, and WorkflowLegacyReminderUpgrade only through scripts/test-installed-upgrade.mjs, which is listed in CAMPAIGN_RUNNERS.

Software gaps:

- Website permission decisions are still stored by Alpha's own code (BrowserSessionStore.permission/setPermission plus AlphaBrowserPlugin.sitePermission). The pinned upstream BrowserSitePermissions class is copied in by stage-local-agent-sources.mjs, but AlphaBrowserPlugin and BrowserSessionStore never call it, so the MVP-11 consolidation is not done.
- MVP-16: two surfaces that open before any enrollment exists still supersede each other.
- The local vendor/eliza checkout is not at the pin (see the stale claims).
- No strict distribution build exists, and one cannot be made until owner decision A-24 is taken: either qualified speech assets are produced or the speech requirement is retired.

Evidence: the only emulator (E) evidence at the pin is one partial round 8 campaign, run on developer APKs with an unpackaged resident runtime. In it WorkflowApprovalNotice and android-workflow-native (14 of 14 cases) passed on both flavors. None of the following has run at the pin: AssistantResidentReuse, the installed-upgrade runner, WorkflowNoticeTap/ProcessDeath, the media classes, ActionJournal or the resident campaign. There is no distribution APK build (B), no AOSP image (I), no real hosted-model integration (R) and no device run (D).

### RELEASE: Release, security, CI and supply chain: privacy redaction, dependency triage, required checks, production bundle audit, exact-head qualification, signed artifacts (MVP-40,44-47)

There is no single RELEASE id in docs/requirements.json. The item maps to AP-14 (status "blocked") and to the "Release" status row shared with AP-02. I checked the release gating code against the ledgers and it is real and tested at source level:
- scripts/release-blockers.mjs names every blocker.
- scripts/verify-apks.mjs runs signer admission against android/release-signer.json, runs the flag-off bundle audit on every APK's assets/public, and requires both variants to share one signer.
- stage-aosp.mjs and provision-unit.mjs refuse non-distributable releases.
- scripts/audit-production-bundle.mjs runs in npm run verify and in CI.
- The dependency-audit snapshot is bound to the lockfile hash. That hash matches the current package-lock.json (1266b6f6...).
- generate-sbom.mjs is deterministic.
- The required-checks ruleset JSON matches the stable job names in the workflows.
- Every action in the workflows is pinned by SHA, and the workflows reference no secrets.
- AlphaLocalAgentPlugin.java:440-441 forces ELIZA_SECRET_SWAP_ENABLED and ELIZA_PII_SWAP_ENABLED to true on native launches. ResidentEgressRedactionInstrumentedTest exists.

Software gaps remain:
- A docs-only change skips "Repository verification", but at least four node tests read files under docs/.
- No Gradle dependency verification metadata exists.
- The SBOM and the vulnerability audit do not cover the resident runtime's ~550 npm packages that ship inside the APK.
- No CI job re-runs the network advisory audit, so new advisories go unnoticed.
- qualify-head.mjs covers verify, the APK build, the bundle audit and the storage specs only. It has no candidate-level aggregation of the emulator and resident campaigns that MVP-47 requires.
- The redaction instrumentation tests only email, card and SSN in a chat turn. MVP-44 also lists vault, OTP and recovery content, selected notes, mail and files, and connection-change paths.
- A stale avatar-initials allowance remains in the bundle audit.
- No HTTPS App Link filter exists. The cloud-delegation callback is still a custom scheme.

Acceptance evidence is close to zero:
- android/release-signer.json has signerSha256 "unset", so no signed artifact exists.
- The "Main required checks" ruleset is not installed, according to the ledger's read-back on 2026-10-10.

- No exact-head qualification result or distribution APK exists at pin 13c9857598.
- The local vendor/eliza checkout is at 3cca1ee4, not the locked 13c98575, so qualify-head would correctly refuse this tree as it stands.

The class label contradicts the row text.

### LOOP-A: Boot, connection choice and retained conversation

I traced main.tsx -> runtime/connection-ui.tsx (connectionController.initialize, admitCloudResident, connectResident, restoreSavedHistory) -> runtime/local-agent.ts and local-agent-native-stream.ts -> AlphaLocalAgentPlugin.java (configureCloudProvider, start, requestStream, cancelStream).

Flag-off Android path: a saved Cloud credential leads to the credit check, configureCloudProvider and Agent.start. The renderer then registers the device, saves selection {kind:'resident'} and restores the saved conversation automatically. Stop gives one later check. With no credential the user sees the Welcome dialog (Sign in, or Use local apps without AI). Remote pairing is retired.

Dev and test-mocks path: the development profile connects through connectDevelopment. History comes back only when the user presses Load conversations -> Restore. Mock mode and fixtures are gated by testMocksEnabled and kept out of production by audit-production-bundle.

The loop is implemented end to end, with no stubs or TODOs in its path. Most steps have class-S coverage: journey-a-conversation.spec.ts (dev profile) and the loop A test in journey-core-loops.production.spec.ts (flag-off renderer with a stubbed Capacitor bridge).

Verified software gaps:
- The flag-off Android interactive first-run sign-in has no test. Every residentCloudLogin click is in a dev-lane spec or starts from a saved credential.
- Browser is not in the production loop A (A-15).
- No contract test checks the context of each view.
- Resident-IPC failure and process-kill cases are missing on the flag-off path.
- Several ledger statements are stale.

Acceptance:
- Class-E results (HOME role, voice permission, NoMockProduct, StartupReadiness, LocalAgentOfflineApps) come from developer APKs with an unpackaged resident runtime.
- No emulator run ever had the resident runtime answering a chat.
- No distribution APK exists. No AOSP image has booted.
- No real Eliza Cloud sign-in or Cerebras inference has run, and there is no device run.
- The hosted resident CI jobs were never dispatched.
- The test-mocks classes (ConnectionChooser) and the voice permission campaigns ran only at the older source 6dcdde34.

### LOOP-B: Voice -> note -> calendar event and reminder (J02)

The code path I traced:
1. Notes recorder in voice-adapter.ts.
2. Transcription: in flag-off builds voice-selection.ts selects the Cloud route; signed out, nothing is captured.
3. The user corrects the transcript and saves the note.
4. "Review transcript with Alpha" (reviewContentQuestion to composeContentQuestion) is the only source of the note's summary and action items.
5. draftFromNote opens a Calendar draft that carries a NoteOrigin. Nothing is written yet.
6. On Save:
   - Reminder: reminder-adapter.ts calls DailyApps.scheduleReminder, then rememberNoteOrigin.
   - Event: calendar-adapter.ts calls calendar.save, then rememberNoteOrigin (event links only off-native).
7. note-origin-adapter.ts shows "From note" and "Open note", and fails closed.

Dev/test-mocks lane (browser): the whole loop runs on real code, fed by fixtures (closed Cloud transcription fixture, synthetic microphone, development agent / Action JSON proposals). It is well covered by journey-b-voice-note-actions.spec.ts and note-calendar-handoff.spec.ts.

Concrete Android bug: note-origin-adapter.ts writes slot 'note-origins:v1:device' through secureConnectionStore.compareExchange. AlphaConnectionPlugin.secureCompareExchange only accepts a hard-coded allow-list of slot names, and this slot is not on it. So on Android every reminder back-link write is rejected, and the user sees "Reminder saved. The link to its note could not be saved." core-loop-status.md says reminder links "use the same store on Android", which is wrong at HEAD. Native event links are disabled by design (needs an upstream per-event identity).

Flag-off production lane: journey-core-loops.production.spec.ts covers only direct Calendar create and delete for loop B. No production spec covers record, Cloud transcript, agent summary, note hand-off, native scheduleReminder or native calendar.save. Its delete step clicks "Delete event" once and asserts the list row is gone. That predates the new "Delete calendar event?" review (c5b29e51), so it is likely vacuous.

Ledger drift: the core-loop-audit loop B count still lists 1 EMULATOR although B-25 was moved to EVIDENCED. The MVP inventory still says J02 is 17 of 23.

Acceptance:
- E evidence exists only for components (CalendarCrud, the reminder one-off and recovery campaigns, the channels campaign, Notes storage), mostly at 6dcdde34 or 2961bd9e on developer-override APKs, not at the current pin.
- No emulator run of the note to reminder or event hand-off exists.
- No R evidence: Cloud transcription and a real agent proposal have not been exercised.
- No I evidence and no D evidence: no spoken note on a phone, audible read-aloud or OS notification on a device.

Software is mostly done but has one real native bug and a missing flag-off test. Acceptance is thin.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/core-loop-status.md loop B says 'Reminder links use the reminder's own id and the same store on Android'. That is false at HEAD: AlphaConnectionPlugin.secureCompareExchange (android/app/src/main/java/ai/elizaresearch/alphaphone/AlphaConnectionPlugin.java ~line 130) has no entry for 'note-origins:v1:device', so every native rememberNoteOrigin call is rejected and the link is never stored.
- docs/core-loop-audit.md loop B count line ('22 evidenced, 0 SOFTWARE, 1 EMULATOR ... 22 of 23 = 96%') does not match its own table. B-25 is now EVIDENCED (round 8), no row is EMULATOR, so it should read 23 evidenced, 0 EMULATOR.
- docs/mvp-remaining-work-2026-10-09.md (~line 745) says 'J02 (loop B) 17 of 23' and 'loop B's six' open emulator steps. Both are stale against the audit (all emulator rows are now evidenced).
- docs/core-loop-audit.md B-28 and MVP-25 describe reminder back-links as working beyond the browser. They only work in the browser build.
- test/browser/journey-core-loops.production.spec.ts loop B clicks 'Delete event' once and asserts the list row count is 0. Since c5b29e51 a 'Delete calendar event?' review opens first, so the assertion likely passes only because the detail view hides the list. The deletion is never confirmed or checked.
- docs/core-loop-audit.md B-15, B-20, B-22, B-26 and B-12 are marked EVIDENCED on class-E runs at 6dcdde34 or earlier, on developer-override APKs at an older upstream pin. No run exists at the current source or pin, and none on distribution APKs.

### LOOP-C: Alarms (Clock handoff, reminders)

The one-time Clock handoff works end to end in source on both of its Android paths, and both fail closed.

1. User panel: clock-adapter.ts → DailyApps.clockHandoff → DailyAppsPlugin.clockHandoff → ClockHandoff.launch. The request is reviewed, recorded in history as "opening" before the handoff, and checked against the time zone, keyguard and focus. The receipt only says "opened". Status after a restart is "unknown". Mock mode is gated by testMocksEnabled, and a flag-off build that asks for mock mode fails closed.
2. Agent: device-actions.ts → agent-adapter.ts → reviewAgentClock (upstream clock-review-executor) → AlphaActionJournalPlugin → ClockAgentReview. The owner reviews in a native AlertDialog. Snooze and dismiss without a target are reduced to SHOW_ALARMS, and the handler is bound to an external component.

Tests: production Playwright loop C (stubbed bridge), journey C (browser Clock), dev-clock, the node contract tests, and the instrumented classes ClockHandoff, ClockRepeatDays and RealClock.

Software gaps:
- Repeating alarms are built in every layer but unreachable in production:
  - DailyAppsPlugin.surfaceInfo never reports clockRepeatDaysVersion.
  - DailyAppsPlugin.clockHandoff rejects a `days` key.
  - Alpha advertises only clock.handoff.v1. At the pin, upstream needs clock.handoff.v2 before it will send `days`.
  - So ClockRepeatDays (E-passed) exercises native code that neither path can reach.
- The local clock-contract.ts has drifted from the upstream pinned contract. Upstream days validation differs, and upstream adds clock_alarm / clock.alarms.v1, which is effectively A-11 option (b). Neither difference is documented.
- test-clock-rendered.mjs (then under scripts; removed in ca3b3fe1) is orphaned.
- In the browser dev profile, an agent repeat request is silently saved as a one-time alarm.
- The web-build browser Clock says "Saved alarms" and rings only while the page is in the foreground, with no on-screen caveat.
- Ledger text is stale.

Acceptance:
- Class E at the round 8 pin (13c98575): ClockHandoff, ClockRepeatDays, ReminderLifecycle and test-reminder-one-off passed.
- RealClock (real AOSP Clock: set, fire, snooze, dismiss) has passed only at round 7 (6dcdde34, older pin, API 35). The fixture is API-35-only, so it failed on API 36.
- ClockAgentReview (denied / CI-1) has never run.
- test-reminder-recovery has not run at the new pin.
- Classes I (an image with DeskClock), R (a real Qwen agent proposing clock_handoff) and D (audible ring, vibration, DND, reboot, DST) have no evidence.
- A-11 is still undecided.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/mvp-remaining-work-2026-10-09.md MVP-27 says 'no run is recorded at the current source' and lists `--classes ClockHandoff,ClockRepeatDays,ReminderLifecycle` as an EMULATOR blocker. Those three classes and test-reminder-one-off passed in round 8 at 2961bd9e/9a1ca161, pin 13c98575 (docs/verification.md). Only RealClock and test-reminder-recovery are unrun at this pin.
- docs/core-loop-audit.md C-3 and C-4 still begin 'No run recorded' and then cite the round 7 pass. The text contradicts itself, and neither row cites the round 8 run, or the round 8 RealClock failure that came from the fixture being limited to API 35.
- docs/core-loop-audit.md C-3 counts ClockRepeatDaysInstrumentedTest as evidence that Android builds correct Clock intents. The repeat-day intent is unreachable in production: DailyAppsPlugin.clockHandoff rejects the `days` key, surfaceInfo never sends clockRepeatDaysVersion, and only clock.handoff.v1 is advertised, so upstream at the pin never sends `days` (it requires clock.handoff.v2). The test only proves a static builder.
- The comment in apps/app/src/prototype/clock-adapter.ts lines 15-17 implies the native handoff can report clockRepeatDaysVersion 1. No native code sets it (DailyAppsPlugin.surfaceInfo), so repeat chips never appear on Android.
- docs/decisions.md A-11 describes option (b), Alpha owning alarms, as hypothetical. The pinned upstream (13c98575 clock-contract.ts) already defines clock_alarm / CLOCK_ALARMS_CAPABILITY 'clock.alarms.v1' ('Eliza owns these durable alarms') and clock.handoff.v2. Neither docs/decisions.md nor docs/architecture.md mentions them.
- apps/app/src/runtime/clock-contract.ts is a local fork that differs from the pinned upstream device-actions/clock-contract.ts in header semantics, days validation (upstream allows an empty or unordered list), clock_alarm support and the v2 capability. docs/architecture.md does not record this ownership split.
- docs/core-loop-status.md C table: the row 'Ring, snooze, ring again, dismiss: yes' refers to the browser Clock only. The note below it says so, but the row could be read as Android evidence.
- Working tree: vendor/eliza is checked out at 3cca1ee4, not the upstream.lock.json pin 13c98575 (git status shows 'M vendor/eliza'). A read of vendor clock code from this checkout does not show the pinned contract. Do not change it, because Codex runs concurrently.

### LOOP-D: Scheduled digests

I traced the loop through the code. Settings and the Home "Latest brief" card both dispatch `alpha:hosted-digests`. `HostedDigestPanel` (apps/app/src/runtime/hosted-digest-ui.tsx) is always mounted from main.tsx. It reads capabilities from `/api/workflow/status`, then manages sources, loops and results through `HostedDigestProtocol` (runtime/hosted-digests.ts) on the selected workflow client. Results land in `createDigestInbox`, which uses the native result inbox on Android and the renderer store elsewhere, and `rememberRetainedDigests` feeds the Home brief.

Flag-on path: browser/development-digests.ts reimplements the agent in the page (snapshot store, tick scheduler, scripted reply). It is gated on `devSurfacesEnabled = DEV && testMocks` and on a development connection. It is covered well by journey-d, dev-digest-schedule, digest-source-renewal and the migration specs.

Flag-off path: it runs against the pinned upstream plugin-workflow (`hostedDigestProtocol`, `hostedDigestSourcePauseProtocol`, and the native-source and evening protocols when the mobile process host configures the native reader). Android delivery goes through ResidentResultTransport and HostedTransport, which allow only the results and ack routes.

Gaps in the flag-off path:
- Its production specs cover only negative states: signed out, and the stub's "Result storage could not be connected". They also cover result delivery into the Home brief. No production-bundle test creates a source or schedule, retries an unconfirmed save, pauses or revokes.
- The Android-only phone-source UI has no Playwright coverage in either profile: choose calendars, `nativeSourceConsent`, renewal tap or notification, the native evening gate, and the on-demand dossier.
- The renderer POSTs `/api/workflow/hosted/dossier`. At pin 13c98575, `workflow-routes.ts` handles that path, but `plugin-routes.ts` does not register it. The upstream test calls `handleWorkflowRoutes` directly, so the route may be unreachable on a real agent. This is plausible, not confirmed.

Acceptance evidence is thin at the current pin:
- D-8 (resident restart check) last ran at 40dbe96b and is host-runtime class S.
- D-14 (HostedProcessRestart) passed only in round 7 at 6dcdde34. The round 8/9 emulator run at 13c98575 built no test-mocks pair.
- D-15 is not dispatched.
- HostedResultNotice passed at the current pin, with two methods skipped.
- No resident digest has run end to end on an emulator or device.
- There is no real-provider or real-model output.
- A-09 is undecided.

Separately, the shared vendor/eliza checkout is at 3cca1ee4, not the locked pin 13c98575. git status shows it modified, possibly by Codex. Files read from the working tree, such as `services/hosted-native-source.ts`, are missing there, so source reads must use `git show <pin>`.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/mvp-remaining-work-2026-10-09.md MVP-38 says the digest restart check ran 'in one host run ... at upstream 352d7a08 ... not repeated at the current pin'. It omits the round 8 rerun at 40dbe96b recorded in core-loop-audit D-8 and verification.md. Neither is at the current pin 13c98575.
- docs/core-loop-audit.md D-14 counts HostedProcessRestart as EVIDENCED from round 7 (6dcdde34, an earlier pin). verification.md 'Emulator results at 2961bd9e' says no test-mocks pair was built at pin 13c98575, so no test-mocks class ran. Per the doc's own rule, that is not evidence for later source.

- docs/core-loop-status.md section D, row 'A missed record does not replace the last real brief': it says Home is asserted on the retained value and 'with an agent connected the Home card lists workflows'. That predates the dedicated Latest brief card (PR 388/MVP-19), which journey D and home-brief-result.production.spec.ts now assert.

### LOOP-E: Browser and credentials

I traced the loop end to end. Renderer: apps/app/src/prototype/browser-adapter.ts. Web build: the AlphaBrowser plugin is BrowserSurface (apps/app/src/browser/browser-surface.ts), registered in browser/register.ts; it uses sandboxed opaque-origin iframes, fetches page text through CORS (reading-source.ts) and speaks with the browser's own voice (reading-review.ts). Android: AlphaBrowserPlugin.java, with CredentialWebView, BrowserReading and BrowserSessionStore. Native read-aloud goes through native-reading-voice.ts to AlphaVoiceCloudPlugin.synthesizeLocalBrowserReading (resident) or synthesizeBrowserReading (paired). Passwords: passwords/password-manager.ts uses the pinned plugin-native-passwords client (ElizaPasswords on Android, dev-vault.ts only when devSurfacesEnabled && browserDevProfile, otherwise null). The flag-off web build says "Available in the Android app". Provider status comes from PasswordProviderSupport.java natively and from browser/password-provider.ts in the dev build.

Both paths are wired. Every renderer call has a native @PluginMethod. vite.config.ts stubs the dev vault, and the built flag-off assets contain no dev-provider strings.

The software gaps:
- The flag-off web build offers "Find in page", but BrowserSurface has no find/findNext/clearFind, so a search after a page loads can only fail with Capacitor's "not implemented on web" text. I read this from the code; no spec exercises it.
- The resident read-aloud path (synthesizeLocalBrowserReading) has no test.
- No installed-APK test covers the integrated vault in Settings (unlock, add, reveal, copy, lock, import/export).
- The BrowserFlow live-search method depends on a live Google page.
- Several browser instrumentation classes have never run.

The biggest product gap is not code. Alpha's own password manager cannot save or fill any website in Alpha's own browser. The shared PasswordFormPolicy needs native per-field origins, and a standard WebView does not provide them. Only Show/Copy works, plus fills by a third-party provider (shown only with the synthetic service in BrowserAutofill). Several ledgers still describe this as "save and fill on a site", "only the Alpha browser is trusted for fills" or a working TOP_ORIGIN fill. Accepting Show/Copy as MVP for P-05/F09/J08 needs an owner decision; the alternative is an engine-level browser adapter.

Acceptance evidence, all class S or E:
- At pin 13c98575 (2961bd9e, developer-override APKs): BrowserSignins, BrowserContinuity and PasswordBrowserFill (refusal only) passed. BrowserFlow failed on live search.
- Not run at this pin: every test-mocks class (BrowserAutofill, BrowserDownload, BrowserFlow upload) and the signin restart campaign. Their only passes are round 7 at the older pin.
- Never run anywhere: the BrowserReading, BrowserIsolatedReading, BrowserSensitiveReading and BrowserPageQuestion classes (no WebView provider with isolated-world injection; CI-2 not dispatched), and BrowserShare, BrowserWebFeatures, BrowserDialogLifecycle, BrowserUnsupportedReading, BrowserStorageFormat and BrowserReadingLive.
- No I, R or D evidence: no release signer, no Proton test vault, no passkeys.

Environment note: the local vendor/eliza checkout is at 3cca1ee4, which does not contain the pin and lacks plugins/plugin-native-passwords, so any build or verify from this checkout will fail to resolve password-manager.ts imports. HEAD still records 13c98575.

PasswordBrowserFillInstrumentedTest.webFormsWithoutNativeFieldOriginsAreRefused asserts the opposite: no offer and no Save for WebView forms. BrowserAutofill fills through the test-mocks SyntheticAutofillService, not the Alpha vault. No app-level test unlocks, saves into or lists the Keystore vault. The row itself notes the native flow was failing before save/unlock.

### LOOP-F: Email and reconnect notifications

Software (source and test, class S): the renderer-side loop is largely built and has strong tests against synthetic providers. That covers reviewed send with From/To/Cc/Bcc/attachments, the durable operation identity, never resending an unknown outcome, refusing a mismatched receipt, invalidating a review when the draft changes, sent-draft cleanup, Trash and undo, paging, grant revocation, owner fencing, hosted-result taps after reload, and honest flag-off states for web and Android. Mock gating holds: the local mailbox (browser/simulated-inbox.ts) installs only under devSurfacesEnabled, and flag-off Inbox runs solely on the managed Cloud routes in cloud-protocol.ts.

What is still missing in software:
- New-mail notifications do not exist. They are blocked on decision A-05.
- Requesting a second Gmail permission may drop one already granted. The scope request is not cumulative, and upstream sets no include_granted_scopes.
- No test drives the successful Reconnect/Authorize Gmail handoff.
- Two smaller behaviors are open: an older version of a sent draft is still offered, and a refused attachment is reported as "Gmail unavailable".
- Several ledger lines are stale.

Acceptance:
- Real integration (R): none. The Google code exchange returns 401 and the managed routes are not deployed.
- Device and user (D): none, and no real send has been authorized.
- Emulator (E) at the current pin 13c98575987a covers InboxOperationJournal, MailAttachment, HostedResultNotice (two methods skipped), the inbox restart campaign and the channels campaign. All of it ran on developer, non-distributable APKs.
- F-15 and F-17 rest on round 7 runs at the previous pin.
- F-16 (process death between notice delivery and tap) has never passed anywhere.

Overall is weighted toward software being near done while R, D and A-05/A-09 gate the loop.

- docs/inbox-provider-completion-plan.md:42-46 says a bounded `in:inbox is:unread` probe runs on cold start, on resume and after leaving Inbox, with a 5-minute floor. That probe (INBOX_PROBE_QUERY from 1fd468b2) is gone from apps/app/src, and scripts/test-inbox-attention-flow.mjs:86-89 now asserts 'Home does not fetch Gmail metadata' and 'resume outside Inbox does not fetch mail'.

HostedBackgroundWorker (test-mocks), the reminder one-off campaign and the notice-denied campaign were not rerun ('No test-mocks pair was built'; reminders and the other permission scenarios are listed as not run). Counting these as EVIDENCED without a pin caveat overstates current evidence.

### J01: Poster -> Calendar (camera/OCR -> event)

I traced the code path. In Camera Scan mode, capturePhoto runs with saveToGallery:false (apps/app/src/prototype/camera-adapter.ts:584-597). The native "Choose image" picker (l.635-640) and the browser import (l.643-652) also lead into openScanReview (scan-review.ts). That calls recognizeLocalText (local-ocr.ts), which uses the pinned Tesseract engine in android/app/src/main/assets/public/ocr through the prepared .eliza/client-features plugin-files local-ocr.ts. From the review, createScanEventReview (scan-event-review.ts) runs suggestScanEventDetails or scanEventDraft (scan-event.ts). That opens owner.open('calendar',{form:draft}), and nothing is written. The only write is Calendar Save (calendar-adapter.ts:278-325): AlphaCalendar.save, or saveOptions/readOptions for all-day, zoned or repeating events with provider readback (AlphaCalendarPlugin.java:56,87). A destination picker exists (calendar-adapter.ts:228).

No development-only or mock code sits in this path. When the flag is off, the same code runs; the web build shows an honest refusal for all-day saves. So the software is close to complete. The real gaps are:
- The unit tests for the inference rules were deleted.
- Scan drafts carry no creationId, so the retained-form store skips them and they are lost on process death.
- No native test runs the whole chain (OCR result -> draft -> CalendarProvider -> Home card).
- Several ledger claims are stale.

The evidence exists only as pieces. Class S: the browser journey and the flag-off spec. Class E, round 8 at 2961bd9e on developer APKs (not distributable): CameraScan, which checks only the no-Photos rule; CalendarAgentCrud direct save and readback; and the WorkflowPhoneNative denied-scope test. Nothing at class E covers the composed native J01 chain. There is no AOSP image (I), no real provider account (R) and no device or physical-poster (D) evidence. The audit's "9 of 9 = 100%" counts these pieces of E evidence as the native loop, which they do not prove.

### J03: Document analysis

The renderer loop is built and works in source. The path is Inbox attachment review/save → Files → "Ask Alpha" (apps/app/src/prototype/selection-adapter.ts → browser/content-question.ts) → api.composeContentQuestion → agent-adapter send → "Review summary note" card (agent-adapter.ts:1058) → summary-note-review.ts → notes with a documentSource (sha256, size and a b1:/a1: reference) → note-source-adapter "Open linked source", which fails closed. The development journey (journey-j03-document-analysis.spec.ts) drives it through rendered controls with the development agent and the development incoming-email simulator. Flag-off coverage (journey-core-loops.production.spec.ts loop J03) checks only the no-agent refusal. No production-bundle test has an agent fixture that answers and leads to the reviewed, source-linked note. On Android the a1: reference methods (AlphaMailAttachmentsPlugin.retainSourceReference/openSourceReference) have no test of any class. The Android Inbox saveReviewed (ACTION_CREATE_DOCUMENT) has no test either. The note reference also depends on a persisted SAF grant that SelectedDocumentAccess releases on Forget or on eviction from the 24 recent files, without regard to notes that link to it. Acceptance: the only class-E evidence at the current pin (13c98575, run 2961bd9e) is the `document` restart campaign on standalone. Launcher was not run there, and HEAD has product changes after that run. That campaign covers only selected-document grant restore and revoke, not note references or the ask/summary chain. The other SelectedDocumentInstrumentedTest methods (Ask compose, PDF rendering, rename, recreation) have no recorded run. There is no R evidence: Gmail is blocked on the grant and there is no real Cerebras/Qwen answer over a document. There is also no I and no D evidence. The audit's "9 of 9 = 100%" leaves out the real-agent step and overstates J03-7.

### J04: Schedule -> travel (maps)

I traced the code path. In the event detail, calendar-adapter.ts:353 `goWhere` calls api.open('maps',{query:where, fromEvent}). Then maps-adapter.ts:193-201 picks up the one-use query in a fenced microtask and calls submit(). The plugin-maps controller (generated into .eliza/client-features from vendor/eliza) then runs search, then an explicit place choice, then Directions, then a manual origin or explicit current location, then planRoute and Start. Start runs foreground guidance plus AlphaNavigationService. "Back to event" (runtime/maps-event-return.ts, maps-adapter.ts:54-67) checks AlphaCalendar.list and restores the event.

Dev/test-mocks path: the whole loop works with a synthetic provider. That provider is injected with page.evaluate(configureMapsProvider), not by the app. The Android emulator gateway (10.0.2.2:47850) is included only when testMocksEnabled. All of this is class S evidence: journey-j04, calendar-maps-handoff, maps-event-return.

Flag-off production path: VITE_MAPS_BASE_URL is unset, so the provider is 'unconfigured'. The event address reaches Maps and the user sees "Connect a Maps provider to search places and plan routes." No route, ETA or navigation can happen. The governing product decision says something else. flow-audit-and-prd.md:40 reads "Search/destination handoff to an installed map application first". The native geo: intent for that exists in DailyAppsPlugin.java:129 (action 'maps'), and native-adapter.ts:260-262 binds it. But maps-adapter.ts replaces qKey/start/dirs and never calls DailyApps.perform({action:'maps'}). So the installed-app handoff the product decision calls for cannot be reached in any build.

The ledger says J04 is "8 of 8 = 100%", but that count leaves out the one step a production user needs: a route or handoff. The flag-off Android renderer path for J04 (stubbed AlphaCalendar with Back to event) has no spec; only the web flag-off build is asserted. Maps instrumentation (MapsRegional, BackgroundNavigation, test-native-recovery permission) is historical class E evidence (Build59, early October), not at pin 13c98575987a. The round 8 emulator campaign did not include Maps. There is no instrumented test of calendar event to Maps to Back to event, and no R or D evidence of any kind.

softwarePct is about 68: the dev loop is solid, but the production fallback, the Android flag-off spec, the gateway-host denylist, and an error-message truthfulness fix are missing. acceptancePct is about 8: only historical E for Maps internals, nothing at the current pin, no R or D. overallPct is 40.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/core-loop-audit.md:412 and docs/mvp-remaining-work-2026-10-09.md:745 say 'J04 8 of 8 = 100%'. That figure leaves out J04-6 (any route or destination for a production user), so it overstates completion. In every flag-off build the loop ends at an unavailable notice.
- docs/core-loop-audit.md:406, docs/decisions.md A-23 and docs/mvp-owner-actions.md:44 present J04-6 as purely an owner decision. But flow-audit-and-prd.md:40 already fixes the MVP Maps behavior as 'handoff to an installed map application first', and the native geo: intent exists (DailyAppsPlugin.java:129). The installed-app handoff (A-23 option c) is unwired software, not only a decision.
- docs/core-loop-audit.md:405 marks J04-5 EVIDENCED partly on 'node scripts/maps/test-native-recovery.mjs permission'. No run at the current pin (13c98575987a) is recorded: the round 8 campaign in docs/verification.md has no Maps cases. The native half is historical class E evidence only.
- docs/core-loop-audit.md:409 / MVP-31 cite MapsRegionalInstrumentedTest and MapsBackgroundNavigationInstrumentedTest as if they apply now. Their last recorded passes are Build59 (docs/maps-regional-validation.md:3), before the current pin.
- docs/core-loop-status.md:269-270 ('No Android run' of the return) is accurate, but the audit's J04-7 EVIDENCED entry does not mention that it is browser-only.
- docs/core-loop-audit.md:404 cites the flag-off check for 'No Maps provider', but journey-core-loops.production.spec.ts asserts it only in the web build, not in the Android-stub flag-off path that the APK ships.
- Flow audit J04 negative case 'Multiple addresses' is taken to mean multiple search results for one location string. Events with several addresses in one location field are not covered.

### J05: Web research -> note

How the loop runs in code:
- Browser menu "Ask about page" (template.html:1425) calls askPage (apps/app/src/prototype/browser-adapter.ts:108).
- Web build: a credential-free CORS re-fetch (browser/reading-source.ts).
- Native: AlphaBrowser.reviewQuestion goes to AlphaBrowserPlugin.reviewPage, then BrowserReading.review(question=true). That step does an isolated-world extract, checks for sensitive pages, shows a native AlertDialog and checks the page has not changed.
- Both paths then go to reviewContentQuestion (browser/content-question.ts), then composeContentQuestion (agent-adapter.ts:855), then send.
- The send matches reviewedSourceDraft. The "Review summary note" card appears only while the VerifiedSession is unchanged (agent-adapter.ts:1058).
- reviewSummaryNote saves exactly one note with webSource. note-web-source-adapter.ts reopens it.

Development profile vs flag-off: the development profile reaches the summary only with a scripted development agent. The flag-off web build with no agent keeps the draft. No mock leaks into the J05 production path.

Gaps:
- Android has no pasted-text fallback when isolated-world reading is unavailable, so J05-5 is web-only. That is the exact condition the round 7 and 8 emulators hit.
- No flag-off Android-stub spec covers the native branch of askPage (the reviewQuestion bounds) or the summary card and save under a resident session.
- No native test covers the answer and save after Send. The test-mocks transport has no connection session.
- The instrumented class depends on example.com.
- Some ledger text is stale or contradicts itself.

Evidence:
- Class S is solid: journey-j05 (2 tests), web-summary-note (7), summary-source-note, and the flag-off production test.
- Class E is partial: J05-2 passed 1 of 4 methods on developer-override APKs in round 7. CI-2 was never dispatched.
- No I, R or D evidence: no real agent (J05-9), no real site, no distributable APK, no device.

Percentages: software 82 because the full path exists and is guarded, with test and Android-fallback gaps. Acceptance 12 because only a sliver of class E evidence exists. Overall 55.

Stale or wrong claims found:

- docs/core-loop-audit.md:144 says BrowserPageQuestionInstrumentedTest 'has not been run'. Round 7 ran it on developer-override APKs: 1 of 4 methods passed and 3 failed because the WebView lacked the isolated world (docs/verification.md:460,641).
- docs/core-loop-audit.md:423 (J05-2 cell) says 'No run recorded.' and then describes the round 7 run in the same cell. The cell contradicts itself.
- docs/mvp-current-status.md:86 says the test 'has no recorded run'. That is stale for the same reason.
- docs/core-loop-status.md J05 table (lines 280-289) omits the round 7 and 8 emulator status. It also implies J05-5 (pasted-text fallback) is a general product property, but on Android a native reviewQuestion rejection only shows a toast and offers no paste (browser-adapter.ts:118-121).
- docs/mvp-remaining-work-2026-10-09.md:745 gives 'J01 6 of 9 closable steps evidenced'. The core-loop-audit table now says J01 9 of 9.
- docs/mvp-remaining-work-2026-10-09.md:747 lists J01-6 and J01-7 as open emulator steps. Round 8 moved both to EVIDENCED (docs/core-loop-audit.md round 8 paragraph).
